page=1 block=886343183 (x=133 y=217) width=3 height=5 fontname=MDGIGQ+URWPalladioL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Abstract: Several epidemiological studies and clinical trials have reported the bene?cial effects of
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=243) width=392 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
green tea, coffee, wine, and curry on human health, with its anti-obesity, anti-cancer, anti-diabetic,
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=256) width=393 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and neuroprotective properties. These effects, which have been supported using cell-based and
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=269) width=392 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
animal studies, are mainly attributed to epigallocatechin gallate found in green tea, chlorogenic
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=282) width=392 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
acid in coffee, resveratrol in wine, and curcumin in curry. Polyphenols are proposed to function via
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=294) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
various mechanisms, the most important of which is related to reactive oxygen species (ROS). These
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=307) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
polyphenols exert con?icting dual actions as anti- and pro-oxidants. Their anti-oxidative actions
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=320) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
help scavenge ROS and downregulate nuclear factor-?B to produce favorable anti-in?ammatory
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=333) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
effects. Meanwhile, pro-oxidant actions appear to promote ROS generation leading to the activation
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=346) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of 5?-AMP-activated protein kinase, which modulates different enzymes and factors with health
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=357) width=392 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
bene?cial roles. Currently, it remains unclear how these polyphenols exert either pro- or anti-oxidant
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=372) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
effects. Similarly, several human studies showedno bene?cial effects of these foods, and, by extension
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=385) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
polyphenols, on obesity. These inconsistencies may be attributed to different confounding study
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=398) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
factors. Thus, this review provides a state-of-the-art update on these foods and their principal
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=411) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
polyphenol components, with an assumption that it prevents obesity.
page=1 block=1716855490 (x=133 y=424) width=274 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Keywords: polyphenols; epigallocatechin-3-O-gallate; chlorogenic acid; resveratrol; curcumin; obe-
page=1 block=1107201631 (x=133 y=449) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sity; reactive oxygen species; 5?-AMP-activated protein kinase; nuclear factor-?B; randomized con-
page=1 block=1107201631 (x=133 y=459) width=394 height=9 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
trolled trial
page=1 block=1107201631 (x=133 y=475) width=45 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
1. Introduction
page=1 block=811792884 (x=133 y=522) width=68 height=6 fontname=MDGIGQ+URWPalladioL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Plant polyphenols are often found in beverages such as tea, wine, and coffee and in
page=1 block=538375433 (x=155 y=538) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
vegetables and fruits such as turmeric, onions, broccoli, apples, berries, citrus fruits, and
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=550) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
plums [1].
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=563) width=44 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Green tea is produced from leaves of the Camellia sinensis plant; a cup of green tea
page=1 block=538375433 (x=155 y=575) width=370 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
brewed from 2.5 g tea leaves has been reported to contain 240–320 mg catechins, of which
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=588) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(?)-epigallocatechin-3-O-gallate (EGCG, Figure 1) accounts for 60%–65% [2,3]. Black tea is
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=600) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
similarlyproduced from these leaves by intrinsic enzymatic processing and microorganisms
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=613) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
during which catechins are polymerized to generate lower EGCG levels.
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=626) width=318 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Coffee contains approximately 2000 different chemicals; the major polyphenols are
page=1 block=538375433 (x=155 y=638) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
chlorogenic acid (CGA, Figure 1) and its derivatives, which account for approximately 3%
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=651) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
w/w of the roasted coffee powder [2–4]. A single cup of coffee may contain 20–675 mg
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=663) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
CGA [3].
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=676) width=39 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Resveratrol (RSV, 3,4?,5-trihydroxystilbene, Figure 1) is often derived from several
page=1 block=538375433 (x=155 y=685) width=370 height=9 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
plant sources, including grapes, peanuts, and berries, and it exists in two isomeric forms:
page=1 block=538375433 (x=133 y=701) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
https://www.mdpi.com/journal/molecules
page=1 block=1864007931 (x=366 y=755) width=157 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
Page: 2 left:1 upper:1
Molecules 2021, 26, x FOR PEER REVIEW 
page=2 block=1512273713 (x=1 y=1) width=155 height=5 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
 
page=2 block=1512273713 (x=1 y=7) width=3 height=6 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
2 of  22 
page=2 block=2099878104 (x=527 y=1) width=31 height=5 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Resveratrol (RSV, 3,4?,5-trihydroxystilbene, Figure 1) is often derived from several 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=162 y=45) width=397 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
plant sources, including grapes, peanuts, and berries, and it exists in two isomeric forms: 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=58) width=419 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
cis- and trans-RSV. Trans-RSV (defined here as RSV, Figure 1) is naturally found in grape 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=72) width=419 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
skin and in the leaf epidermis of the grape vine and is the main RSV form in red grape 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=85) width=419 height=8 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
juice (3.38 mg/L) [1,4]. Daily RSV intake may be in the order of several mg/day [5]. 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=99) width=387 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
Curcumin (CRC, Figure 1) is a yellow pigment, an active component of the turmeric 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=162 y=113) width=397 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
plant (Curcuma longa), and is widely used in cooking, cosmetics, dyes, and medicines [1,6]. 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=126) width=419 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
In Asian populations, approximately 100 mg CRC is consumed daily [1,4]. 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=140) width=349 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
Several studies have shown that consuming tea, coffee, wine, and curry can be bene-
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=162 y=153) width=394 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
ficial in fighting against various diseases including, cancer, obesity, neurodegenerative 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=167) width=419 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
Molecules2021,26,45d3isorders, and diabetes. Their polyphenolic constituents are believed to contribute mainly 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=69 y=176) width=490 height=12 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
to these effects as discussed in our previous reviews [1–3,6,7]. 
page=2 block=1778422985 (x=140 y=194) width=290 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5

Several observational human studies have indicated that tea or green tea consumption
page=2 block=2025928493 (x=222 y=796) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
can exert bene?cial effects on obesity. For example, a cross-sectional survey of 1210
page=2 block=2025928493 (x=200 y=808) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
epidemiologically sampled adults (569 men and 641 women) demonstrated that habitual
page=2 block=2025928493 (x=200 y=821) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tea drinkers (455 individuals were green or oolong tea consumers, and 18 were black
page=2 block=2025928493 (x=200 y=833) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tea consumers) for >10 years showed a 19.6% reduction in percent body fat and a 2.1%
page=2 block=2025928493 (x=200 y=846) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
reduction in waist-to-hip ratios when compared with non-habitual tea drinkers [8].

2.1. Observational Epidemiological Studies of Anti-Obesity Effects of Green Tea/EGCG
page=2 block=1513071903 (x=200 y=780) width=351 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 3 left:1 upper:1
Molecules 2021, 26, 453
page=3 block=1905548224 (x=1 y=1) width=78 height=5 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
3 of 22
page=3 block=580965610 (x=501 y=1) width=23 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Recent data from an epidemiological study with 6472 adult participants found that
page=3 block=1240328647 (x=154 y=36) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tea consumers had a lower mean waist circumference (WC) and lower body mass index
page=3 block=1240328647 (x=132 y=48) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(BMI) (25 vs. 28 kg/m2 in men; 26 vs. 29 kg/m2 in women) than non-consumers [1].
page=3 block=1240328647 (x=132 y=59) width=370 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
However, a recent study on 3539 participants observed that green tea was not asso-
page=3 block=1240328647 (x=154 y=73) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ciated with visceral obesity or metabolic syndrome (MetS) [9]. Thus, further studies are
page=3 block=1240328647 (x=132 y=86) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
required to elucidate the anti-obesity properties of green tea.
page=3 block=1240328647 (x=132 y=98) width=266 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2.2. Human Intervention Studies on the Effects of Green Tea/EGCG on Obesity
page=3 block=1858976440 (x=132 y=121) width=319 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Several intervention studies have reported the bene?cial effects of green tea [10,11]. In
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=136) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
a randomized controlled trial (RCT) on 35 subjects with obesity and MetS, patients were
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=149) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
randomly assigned to a control (four cups of water/day), green tea (four cups/day), or
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=161) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
green tea extract (GTE) (two capsules and four cups of water/day) group for 8 weeks [12].
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=174) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
As per the ?ndings of this study, it showed that green tea and GTE signi?cantly decreased
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=187) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
body weight and BMI vs. controls at 8 weeks (?2.5 ± 0.7 kg and ?1.9 ± 0.6, respectively).
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=198) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
These results further indicated the therapeutic effects of green tea catechins (GTCs) in
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=212) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
improving MetS characteristics in obese patients.
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=224) width=216 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A double-blind parallel multicenter trial in 240 subjects by Nagao et al. [13] observed
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=237) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
that participants who consumed a GTC 583 mg/day dose exhibited greater decreases
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=249) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in body weight, BMI, body fat ratio, body fat mass, WC, hip circumference, visceral fat
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=262) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
area (VFA), and subcutaneous fat area when compared to those who consumed a GTC
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=274) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
96 mg/day dose.
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=287) width=75 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The anti-obesity bene?ts of green tea were also demonstrated in a meta-analysis of
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=300) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
24 human studies [14]. In total, 5 of the 11 trials in Eastern populations showed signi?cant
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=312) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
weight loss, ranging from 1 kg to 2 kg, and 3 of the 13 trials also demonstrated notable
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=325) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
weight loss ranging from 1 kg to 9 kg.
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=337) width=166 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In another RCT, 102 obese women were randomly divided into 2 groups: high-dose
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=350) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
green tea group (EGCG, 856.8 mg/day) and the placebo group. The results indicated
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=362) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that 12 weeks of high-dose green tea resulted in signi?cant weight loss (76.8 ± 11.3 kg–
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=374) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
75.7 ± 11.5 kg) and decreased BMI, WC, total cholesterol (TC) and plasma low-density
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=386) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lipoprotein (LDL) levels, without any adverse effects to participants [15].
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=400) width=320 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In another RCT, 73 obese women were divided into two groups: group A received
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=412) width=370 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
GTE supplements for the ?rst 6 weeks, while group B received daily placebo. After 6 weeks
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=425) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of treatment and a 14-day washout period, groups A and B were switched to placebo
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=438) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
treatment and GTE treatment mutually for 6 weeks. These results showed that GTE
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=450) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
effectively increased leptin and reduced LDL levels in these women [16].
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=463) width=319 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Individuals with Down syndrome traditionally experience higher obesity rates [17].
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=475) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In a double-blind phase II clinical trial in 77 young adults with Down syndrome, the
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=488) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
placebo group showed increased body weight and BMI, which were not noted in an EGCG-
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=500) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
treated group. The effect of EGCG on body composition was mainly observed in males,
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=513) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
with signi?cant differences between EGCG and placebo groups after 12 months, for body
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=525) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
weight (estimated adjusted mean difference (AMD): ?2.34, 95% con?dence interval (CI):
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=537) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
?4.21, ?0.48) and body fat (AMD: ?1.23, CI: ?2.43, ?0.04), suggesting that EGCGs were
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=550) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
bene?cial for weight management in patients with Down syndrome [18].
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=563) width=320 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A recent systematic review and meta-analysis revealed that green tea supplementation
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=576) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
had favorable effects on body weight (weighted mean difference (WMD): ?1.78 kg, CI:
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=587) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?2.80, ?0.75) and BMI (WMD: ?0.65 kg/m2, CI: ?1.04, ?0.25). The reduction in WC after
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=599) width=392 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
green tea consumption was determined to be signi?cant for subjects using green tea at
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=613) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?800 mg/day (WMD: ?2.06 cm) and treatment durations of <12 weeks (WMD: ?2.39 cm).
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=625) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
This dose-response observation indicated that green tea intake at <500 mg/day may reduce
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=638) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
body weight over 12 weeks of treatment [19].
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=651) width=199 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In contrast, several clinical trials failed to show body weight reduction via green tea
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=154 y=664) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
consumption. A clinical trial in 151 participants aged 30–70 years showed that 1.8 g/day
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=676) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
GTE consumption for 12 weeks did not lower body weight when compared with placebo,
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=689) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
although a signi?cant LDL-cholesterol (LDL-C) lowering effect was noted [20]. Data from
page=3 block=1037346554 (x=132 y=701) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 4 left:1 upper:1
Molecules 2021, 26, 453

4 of 22
page=4 block=1064456966 (x=501 y=1) width=23 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
an RCT of 937 healthy postmenopausal women who received decaffeinated GTE containing
page=4 block=1013528106 (x=132 y=36) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

weight, BMI, or WC; further, it did not alter energy intake or mean hormone concentrations
page=4 block=1013528106 (x=132 y=61) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
for over 12 months [21].
page=4 block=1013528106 (x=132 y=73) width=105 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2.3. Laboratory Studies and Mechanisms of EGCG Action
page=4 block=605201451 (x=132 y=96) width=234 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Obesity is often characterized by the excessive accumulation of triglycerides (TGs),
page=4 block=72615125 (x=154 y=111) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
which are hepatically synthesized from both fatty acids (FAs) and de novo lipogenesis.
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=124) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Fatty acid synthase (FASN) is a central enzyme in lipogenesis and is responsible for FA
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=136) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
production. The enzyme has gained considerable attention as a potential therapeutic
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=149) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
target for obesity and cancer [22,23]. Zhang et al. [24] found that GTE inhibited FASN
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=161) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
activity from duck liver, with a half-maximal inhibitory concentration (IC50) of 12.2 mg dry
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=174) width=393 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
weight/mL [25]. Similarly, Tian et al. reported that EGCG inhibited FASN, with an IC50 of
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=187) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
52 µM, mainly via interactions with the FASN ?-ketoacyl reductase domain.
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=199) width=335 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Using a high-fat diet (HFD)-induced mouse obesity model, Wolfram et al. [26] ob-
page=4 block=72615125 (x=154 y=212) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
served that 1% (w/w) TEAVIGO, which contains 97.69% EGCG, prevented HFD-induced
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=224) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
increases in mouse body weight. The authors also showed that FASN and acetyl-CoA
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=237) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
carboxylase (ACC) mRNA levels were markedly decreased in adipose tissue. ACC has been
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=249) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
identi?ed as a key lipogenesis enzyme [27] and is potentially an anti-obesity target [28].
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=262) width=385 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Both FASN and ACC expressions have been positively regulated by upstream lipoge-
page=4 block=72615125 (x=154 y=274) width=372 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
nesis transcription factors, including CCAAT/enhancer-binding proteins (C/EBPs) [29],
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=287) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
peroxisome proliferation-activated receptors (PPARs, especially PPAR?) [30], and sterol
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=300) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
regulatory element-binding proteins (SREBPs) [31]. Lee et al. demonstrated that 0.2% or
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=312) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
0.5% (w/w) EGCG can reduce body weight and the mass of several adipose tissues in
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=325) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a dose-dependent manner in an HFD-induced mouse obesity model [32]. These results
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=337) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
indicated that EGCG lowered plasma TG and liver lipid levels. In the epidermal white
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=350) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adipose tissue of EGCG-treated mice, C/EBP, PPAR?, and SREBP mRNA levels were
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=362) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
observed to be signi?cantly decreased [32].
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=375) width=189 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
5?-AMP-activated protein kinase (AMPK) has been identi?ed as a metabolite-sensing
page=4 block=72615125 (x=154 y=384) width=370 height=9 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
protein kinase. Activated AMPK triggers bene?cial physiological effects, including re-
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=400) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ductions in fat deposition. Ha et al. revealed that AMPK knockdown has upregulated
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=412) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
fat-forming enzymes, including FASN, ACC, and stearoyl-CoA desaturase, which is an-
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=425) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
other key enzyme of fat synthesis. These results suggest that activated AMPK inhibited fat
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=438) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
synthesis by inhibiting lipogenesis [33]. Given that GTE and EGCG activated AMPK by
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=450) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
inducing reactive oxygen species (ROS) generation [34,35], GTE and EGCG could improve
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=463) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
body weight and lipid metabolism by activating AMPK through ROS generation. EGCG
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=475) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
actions against lipogenesis and adipogenesis are shown in Figure 2.
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=488) width=297 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Increased lipolysis can hydrolyze TGs and release free FAs, leading to anti-obesity
page=4 block=72615125 (x=154 y=500) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
effects. Treatment of pre-adipocyte 3T3-L1 cells with 10 µM EGCG for 24 h has been found
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=513) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to decrease intracellular lipid accumulation. Under the same experimental conditions,
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=525) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
increased glycerol levels in the medium were observed, and hormone-sensitive lipase
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=538) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(HSL) mRNA levels, which catalyze rate-limiting stages in the hydrolysis of stored TGs to
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=551) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
monoacylglycerol and free FAs, were similarly increased [36].
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=563) width=271 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Consistent with these ?ndings, Wistar rats on an obesity induced 8-week cafeteria
page=4 block=72615125 (x=154 y=576) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
diet, and supplemented with GTE (500 mg/kg body weight at 5 days/week for 12 weeks),
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=588) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
led to signi?cant reductions in obesity indicators, e.g., hyperlipidemia, fat synthesis, body
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=601) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
weight, and fat depots when compared with the control diet group. Importantly, repression
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=613) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of de novo lipogenesis in adipose tissue, reduced lipid droplets in the liver, and insulin resis-
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=626) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tance development in diet-induced obese rats were accompanied by AMPK activation [37].
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=638) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Similarly, mice fed a HFD with EGCG (50 and 100 mg/kg/day) exhibited signi?cantly
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=651) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
increased HSL mRNA expression in white adipose tissue when compared with the HFD
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=664) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
group, and AMPK activity was noted to increase in both subcutaneous and epididymal
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=676) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adipose tissues in these mice [38]. These results suggest that GTE/EGCG decreased obesity
page=4 block=72615125 (x=132 y=689) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in rats and mice via AMPK activation (Figure 2).

(SOD) levels. EGCG also increased the expression of sirtuin-1 (SIRT1), catalase, fatty acid-

page=5 block=1841099284 (x=137 y=194) width=416 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
These findings are represented by molecular events in Figure 2. 
page=5 block=1841099284 (x=137 y=207) width=297 height=7 fontname=NJAAHI+PalatinoLinotype wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5

induced mice, causing a 1.4–1.9-fold increase in PPAR? mRNA levels when compared to

It is also possible that EGCG may have exerted its anti-obesity effects by reducing
page=5 block=1130258687 (x=166 y=926) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lipid uptake and transport. The multi-ligand receptor, cluster of differentiation 36 (CD36),

port [44]. EGCG decreased TCs and TGs in mice given 1,3-dichloro-2-propanol (1 mg/kg

body weight/day), which is an inducer of oxidative stress. EGCG dramatically increased

the expression of phosphorylated AMPK at Thr172 and lowered CD36 expression [45].

CD36 has also been deemed essential for lipoprotein binding at the liver [46]. Therefore,
page=5 block=1130258687 (x=144 y=1001) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
decreased CD36 expression induced by EGCG may be related to changes in blood lipid
page=5 block=1130258687 (x=144 y=1014) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
pro?les [45]. Thus, EGCG inhibition of CD36 via AMPK activation may contribute to its
page=5 block=1130258687 (x=144 y=1026) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
anti-obesity effects [47].

3. The Effects of Coffee/CGA on Obesity
page=6 block=556662073 (x=132 y=36) width=185 height=6 fontname=MDGIGQ+URWPalladioL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Coffee and CGA have been determined to exert anti-obesity effects in humans [48],
page=6 block=170778406 (x=154 y=51) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with several observational and experimental epidemiological studies supporting this
page=6 block=170778406 (x=132 y=64) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
hypothesis.
page=6 block=170778406 (x=132 y=76) width=51 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
3.1. Epidemiological Studies on Coffee/CGA
page=6 block=1708490318 (x=132 y=99) width=178 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A cross-sectional study in 137 patients with non-alcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD)
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=154 y=114) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and 108 controls showed that coffee consumption was inversely associated with obesity
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=127) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and insulin resistance [1,49]. The results of a Mendelian randomization study in 93,179 indi-
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=139) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
viduals demonstrated that coffee intake of up to four cups/day was associated with a lower
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=152) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
risk of obesity with an odds ratios (ORs) of 0.82–0.86, when compared with non-coffee
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=164) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
drinkers [50].
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=177) width=59 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Yonekura et al. [51] conducted a cross-sectional study in 232 Japanese women aged
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=153 y=190) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
40–65 years and found that daily coffee consumption was inversely associated with high BMI
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=202) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(adjusted OR: 0.14, CI: 0.14, 0.96) and body fat percentage (adjusted OR: 0.33, CI: 0.14, 0.82).
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=215) width=389 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Recently, Koyama et al. [9] reported that coffee consumption is associated with signif-
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=154 y=227) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
icantly lower levels of visceral obesity (OR: 0.746, CI: 0.588, 0.947) and MetS (OR: 0.706,
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=240) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
CI: 0.565, 0.882). These ?ndings indicate the bene?cial effects of coffee consumption
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=252) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
toward obesity.
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=265) width=68 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
However, several studies failed to show such favorable effects. For example, an epi-
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=154 y=277) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
demiological study in 17,953 Korean adults aged 19–65 years failed to show that coffee con-
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=290) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sumption has health bene?ts; the OR for obesity of those who drank coffee ?3 times/day
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=302) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
was 1.37 (CI: 1.15, 1.63) when compared with those who had coffee <1 time/week [52].
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=315) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Similarly, an epidemiological study in 5995 women indicated that the ORs of high coffee
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=328) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
consumption (?3 cups) were positively associated with obesity as measured by BMI (OR:
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=339) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2.52, CI: 1.91, 3.34) and abdominal obesity as measured by WC (OR: 2.11, CI: 1.59, 2.79)
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=353) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
when compared with non-coffee drinkers [53]. Coffee with additives such as sugar was
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=365) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
positively correlated with the prevalence of obesity.
page=6 block=1840217399 (x=132 y=378) width=226 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
3.2. Clinical Studies on Coffee/CGA
page=6 block=598049166 (x=132 y=400) width=146 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A systemic review of three RCTs, including 142 participants, observed a signi?cant
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=154 y=416) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
difference in body weight in a green coffee extract (GCE)-consuming group when compared
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=428) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with placebo (mean difference: ?2.47 kg, CI: ?4.23, ?0.72) [54].
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=440) width=280 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A 12-week RCT in 30 overweight individuals reported that the average body weight
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=154 y=453) width=370 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
loss in subjects who consumed CGA-enriched coffee was 5.4 kg, while in the instant
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=466) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
coffee groups, this was 1.7 kg, suggesting a bene?cial effect of CGA on body weight
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=479) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
management [55].
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=491) width=79 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Haidari et al. [56] conducted an 8-week RCT where 64 obese women were divided
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=154 y=504) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
into the intervention group (receiving 400 mg GCE, equivalent to 180 mg CGA, n = 30) or
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=516) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the control placebo group (receiving 400 mg starch, n = 34). As per the study results, signif-
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=529) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
icant reductions were noted in body weight, BMI, and fat mass indices, and waist-to-hip
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=541) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
circumference ratios in both groups; however, the decrease was higher in the intervention
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=554) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
group. In addition, serum TC, LDL, leptin, and plasma free FA levels were signi?cantly
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=566) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
decreased in the intervention group, after adjusting for energy and ?ber intake.
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=579) width=349 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In another RCT, 142 healthy overweight men and women were divided into two
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=154 y=592) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
groups: the high CGA group (369 mg CGA/serving) and the control coffee group (35 mg
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=604) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
CGA/serving). Coffee was consumed once daily for 12 weeks, with 4-week pre- and
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=617) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
post-observation periods. Body weight, BMI, VFA, total abdominal fat area (TFA), and WC
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=629) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
were all signi?cantly decreased in the high CGA group when compared with the control
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=642) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
group. Changes in VFA (?9.0 ± 13.9 cm2 in the CGA group vs. ?1.0 ± 14.3 cm2 in the
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=652) width=392 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
control) and TFA (?13.8 ± 22.9 cm2 in the CGA group vs. ?2.0 ± 16.2 cm2 in the control)
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=665) width=393 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
from baseline to 12 weeks were found to be signi?cantly higher in the high CGA group
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=679) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
than in the control group [57].
page=6 block=1192603187 (x=132 y=692) width=132 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 7 left:1 upper:1
Molecules 2021, 26, 453
page=7 block=167550865 (x=1 y=1) width=78 height=5 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
7 of 22
page=7 block=531373404 (x=501 y=1) width=23 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
An 8-week RCT was conducted in patients with NAFLD; patients were divided into
page=7 block=277497558 (x=154 y=36) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
an intervention group (400 mg green GCE containing 100 mg CGA, n = 24) or a placebo
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=48) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
group (n = 24). The authors observed that GCE supplementation signi?cantly reduced
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=61) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
BMI (mean difference (MD): ?0.57, CI: ?0.84, ?0.29) and increased serum high-density
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=72) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lipoprotein cholesterol (HDL-C) levels (MD: 7.06, CI: 0.25, 13.87) when compared with
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=86) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the placebo group. Serum TC decreased signi?cantly in the GCE group (MD: ?13.33, CI:
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=97) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?26.04, ?0.61) [58].
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=110) width=87 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
However, an RCT in 18 healthy male subjects who consumed 185 mL of a test beverage
page=7 block=277497558 (x=154 y=123) width=370 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with or without CGAs (329 mg) per day for 4 weeks showed no effects on body weight,
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=136) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
BMI, or body fat, although a signi?cantly higher postprandial energy expenditure was
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=149) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
observed in the CGA group when compared with the controls [59]. Thus, further studies
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=161) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
are warranted to determine the effects of coffee and CGAs on obesity.
page=7 block=277497558 (x=132 y=174) width=305 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
3.3. Laboratory Studies and Mechanisms of CGA Action
page=7 block=2055472834 (x=132 y=196) width=228 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Several basic studies have generated evidence to support the bene?cial effects of CGA
page=7 block=392760852 (x=154 y=212) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on obesity. Cho et al. demonstrated that 0.02% (w/w) CGA supplementation can lower
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=224) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
body weight and TG levels in plasma, liver, and heart in a HFD-induced mouse obesity
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=237) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
model [60]. These authors also showed that CGA signi?cantly inhibited liver-based FASN
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=249) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
when compared with the high-fat group. In accordance with these ?ndings, Huang et al.
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=262) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
observed that 90 mg/kg CGA consumption suppressed HFD-induced increases in body
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=274) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
weight, and downregulated FASN and ACC mRNA expression in rats [61].
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=287) width=330 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In a rat study, CGA supplementation (~100 mg/kg/day) reduced in?ammation and fat
page=7 block=392760852 (x=154 y=300) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
deposition in the liver, along with reduced plasma liver enzyme activities in diet-induced
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=312) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
obese rats [62]. The inhibitory effects of fat-forming enzymes by CGA may be in part due
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=325) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to the regulation of upstream lipogenesis transcription factors. Wang et al. demonstrated
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=337) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that mice fed a HFD with CGA (150 mg/kg) had signi?cantly lowered body weight in
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=350) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
comparison to HFD-fed control mice. In epididymal adipose tissue, CGA signi?cantly
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=362) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
decreased FASN expression and expression of the upstream transcription factors, C/EBP,
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=375) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
PPAR?, and SREBP, but it increased PPAR? expression [63]. These ?ndings agreed with
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=387) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the previous HFD-induced obesity studies in murine models [61,64], suggesting CGA
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=400) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
improved metabolic homeostasis.
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=412) width=148 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Additionally, CGA may reduce lipogenesis via AMPK activation, thereby inhibiting
page=7 block=392760852 (x=154 y=425) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
fat-forming enzymes [65]. In HepG2 cells, CGA (0.5–10 mM) induced AMPK activation
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=438) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in a dose- and time-dependent manner (0.5–24 h). Activated AMPK then inhibited fat
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=450) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
synthesis by inhibiting fat-forming enzymes and lipogenesis transcription factors [33].
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=463) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Hou et al. also demonstrated that CGA-treatment (250 µM and 1000 µM) for 24 h can
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=475) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
induce ROS production in human colon cancer cells (HCT116 and HT26) [66]. Since AMPK
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=488) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
was activated by ROS [67], CGA could regulate lipid metabolism via AMPK activation
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=500) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
through ROS generation. Figure 2 outlines several CGA actions.
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=513) width=282 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The ability of CGA to increase lipolysis may be associated with its anti-obesity effects.
page=7 block=392760852 (x=154 y=525) width=372 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Flanagan et al. examined the long-term health bene?ts of CGA. They observed that CGA
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=538) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
consumption for 192 h increased lipolysis, as measured by free FAs and glycerol [68]. In
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=551) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
3T3-L1 cells, 20 µM CGA increased lipolysis by upregulating HSL expression [69,70]. These
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=563) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
results suggested that CGA promoted lipid digestion by upregulating lipase expression.
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=576) width=387 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Xu et al. [71] reported that mice fed a HFD supplemented with CGA and caffeine had
page=7 block=392760852 (x=154 y=588) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
signi?cantly increased HSL and AMPK mRNA expression in the liver, when compared
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=601) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
with the HFD group. CGA may also upregulate enzymes involved in FA ?-oxidation,
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=613) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
which in turn facilitates hepatic lipid degradation by activating PPAR? in the liver and
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=626) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adipose tissue [72]. These results indicated that CGA decreased obesity by enhancing
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=638) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lipolysis via AMPK activation [71] (Figure 2).
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=651) width=199 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Ma et al. [64] reported that mice fed a HFD supplemented with CGA (100 mg/kg,
page=7 block=392760852 (x=154 y=664) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
twice a week) signi?cantly suppressed the hepatic expression of CD36 when compared
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=676) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to the HFD group. Given that AMPK signaling plays a key role in CD36-induced lipid
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=689) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
absorption [47], CGA may inhibit CD36 via AMPK activation, leading to reduced lipid
page=7 block=392760852 (x=132 y=701) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 8 left:1 upper:1
Molecules 2021, 26, 453
page=8 block=1750120280 (x=1 y=1) width=78 height=5 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
8 of 22
page=8 block=707719282 (x=501 y=1) width=23 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
uptake and transport. CGA may also affect lipid digestion by suppressing bile acid function,
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=132 y=36) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
which is deemed critical for the digestion and absorption of lipids at the small intestine.
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=132 y=48) width=386 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The microRNA-122 (miR-122) is liver speci?c and plays a critical role in liver home-
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=154 y=61) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ostasis [73]; its inhibition is associated with the gene suppression of key roles in liver lipid
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=132 y=73) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
metabolism, such as FASN [74]. Murase et al. demonstrated that CGA increased miR-122
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=132 y=86) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
levels in Hepa1–6 cells [75]. These results suggested that CGA may inhibit lipogenesis
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=132 y=98) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
through post-transcriptional mechanisms.
page=8 block=1883652579 (x=132 y=111) width=185 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
4. The Effects of Wine/RSV on Obesity
page=8 block=788592721 (x=132 y=133) width=177 height=6 fontname=MDGIGQ+URWPalladioL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
4.1. Observational Epidemiological Studies on the Anti-Obesity Effects of Wine/RSV
page=8 block=1107779742 (x=132 y=147) width=342 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Epidemiological studies have suggested that light to moderate consumption of alcohol
page=8 block=527939020 (x=154 y=162) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
may have protective effects against obesity [1]. A prospective cohort study which enrolled
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=175) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
15,920 normal-weight (BMI: 18.5 kg/m2 to <25 kg/m2) postmenopausal women observed
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=185) width=392 height=9 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
that the risk of becoming overweight and obese over a 7-year follow-up period was 35%
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=200) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and 88% lower, respectively, for women in the upper quintile of alcohol intake, relative
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=213) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to non-drinkers [76]. Of the alcoholic drinks, wine consumption showed the greatest
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=225) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
inverse association for the risk of being overweight (hazard ratio (HR): 0.75, CI, 0.68, 0.84),
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=238) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
followed by liquor (HR: 0.85, CI: 0.78, 0.93) and beer (HR: 0.90, CI: 0.82, 1.00). Vidot
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=250) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
et al. [77] reported that among wine drinkers, low and moderate drinkers had a lower OR
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=263) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for MetS when compared with non-drinkers (OR: 0.72, CI: 0.55, 0.96 and OR: 0.43, CI: 0.21,
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=275) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
0.87, respectively). However, no signi?cant associations were found for heavy wine (OR:
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=288) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
1.16, CI: 0.43, 3.16) and liquor drinkers.
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=300) width=172 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Inan-Eroglu et al. [78] examined the associations between types of alcoholic drinks
page=8 block=527939020 (x=154 y=313) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and adiposity in a large United Kingdom cohort (n = 280,183, 48.3% female). Study data
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=325) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
indicated that when compared to non-wine drinkers, red wine, champagne, and forti?ed
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=338) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
wine drinkers had lower BMIs (differences were as follows: ?0.75 kg/m2, CI: ?0.78,
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=348) width=394 height=9 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
?0.72 kg/m2; ?0.48 kg/m2, CI: ?0.52, ?0.45 kg/m2; and ?0.24 kg/m2, CI: ?0.29, ?0.18
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=361) width=392 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
kg/m2, respectively). Beer and spirits drinkers were determined to have higher BMIs when
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=374) width=392 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
compared with non-beer and spirit drinkers (difference: 0.18 kg/m2, CI: 0.14, 0.22 kg/m2,
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=386) width=394 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and difference: 0.64 kg/m2, CI: 0.61, 0.68 kg/m2, respectively). A population-based study
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=399) width=393 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
by Osella et al. showed that >10 g/day of wine consumption was associated with being
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=413) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
overweight [79].
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=426) width=72 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A cross-sectional study in healthy volunteers (1481 women aged 35–60 years and 1210
page=8 block=527939020 (x=154 y=438) width=370 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
men aged 45–60 years) showed a J-shaped relationship of waist-to-hip ratios and BMIs,
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=451) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in terms of wine consumption [80]. When compared with non-drinkers, men consuming
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=464) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
<100 g/day wine had a lower BMI, and lower waist-to-hip ratio was found in both men
page=8 block=527939020 (x=131 y=476) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and women.
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=489) width=55 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
These ?ndings suggest that low and moderate wine consumption may exert bene?cial
page=8 block=527939020 (x=154 y=501) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
effects on obesity. However, a follow-up survey of 8103 subjects with a mean age of
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=514) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
35.4 years during ?6 years failed to show such bene?cial effects; instead, results showed a
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=525) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
non-signi?cant association between wine consumption and MetS [81].
page=8 block=527939020 (x=132 y=539) width=308 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
4.2. Human Intervention Studies Investigating the Effects of Wine/RSV on Obesity
page=8 block=1568450200 (x=132 y=561) width=337 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Intervention studies have reported on the obesity-related effects of wine consumption.
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=154 y=577) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In an RCT in obese subjects who habitually consumed moderate alcohol levels, 40 of
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=589) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
49 eligible participants (BMI, 34.2 ± 6.4 kg/m2) completed the 3-month intervention study
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=600) width=393 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on a 1500 kcal dietary regimen, one with 10% of energy from white wine and one with 10%
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=615) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of energy from grape juice [82]. These results showed that all subjects achieved signi?cant
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=627) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
body weight reduction; percent body fat, WC, blood pressure, blood glucose, insulin, TGs,
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=640) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and cholesterol were also reduced. In contrast, another RCT revealed that moderate wine
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=652) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
consumption in overweight women did not improve insulin sensitivity and any other
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=665) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
correlates of insulin sensitivity, i.e., body weight, blood lipids, and blood pressure [83].
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=677) width=381 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In contrast to the studies focusing on wine consumption, the potential health bene-
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=154 y=690) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?ts of RSV consumption have also been examined. Following a literature search, Wang
page=8 block=1972772630 (x=132 y=702) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=9 block=773300030 (x=501 y=1) width=23 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
et al. [4] summarized ?ve RCTs published between 2009 and 2013, of which four reported

favorable RSV effects. These included the downregulation of in?ammatory markers, such

page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=86) width=265 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In a comprehensive clinical study review, Wahab et al. [84] discussed the effects of RSV,
page=9 block=384283650 (x=154 y=98) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and included 17 studies in patients with chronic diseases and 21 studies in healthy subjects.
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=111) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
These authors reported the bene?cial effects of RSV, including reduced body weight,
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=123) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
BMI, fat weight, LDL-C levels, and adipocyte size, in addition to the downregulation of
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=136) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in?ammatory cytokines such as TNF-?. The authors also recorded elevated adiponectin
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=149) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
levels and adipose tissue lipolysis.
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=161) width=152 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In a recent review, Singh et al. [85] summarized a number of RSV clinical trials in
page=9 block=384283650 (x=154 y=174) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
patients with obesity, diabetes, cancers, MetS, Alzheimer’s disease, cardiovascular disease,
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=186) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and in?ammatory disease. These authors observed that RSVs exerted favorable effects in
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=199) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
13 of the 15 studies in patients with obesity, being overweight, and MetS.
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=211) width=320 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In contrast, several studies published con?icting results in this area. For example,

a study by Poulsen et al. [86] showed that the consumption of RSV (500 mg for 28 days)

by 24 obese, but otherwise healthy males, resulted in non-signi?cant changes in terms

of obesity markers, total body mass, total body fat mass, and visceral and abdominal
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=262) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
subcutaneous fat volumes. Meanwhile, Gualdoni et al. [87] reported that 10 healthy
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=274) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
volunteers who consumed 5 g RSV showed a signi?cant increase in TNF-? levels at
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=287) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
24 h after treatment when compared at baseline. Peripheral blood mononuclear cells or

isolated monocytes showed that RSV potentiated TNF-? production stimulated by different
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=312) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Toll-like receptor agonists. Moreover, signi?cant increases in NF-?B activities and p105
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=324) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
phosphorylation were indicative of alternative NF-?B pathway activation.
page=9 block=384283650 (x=132 y=337) width=326 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
4.3. Laboratory Studies and RSV Mechanisms
page=9 block=1397516135 (x=132 y=359) width=187 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Most cell-based and animal studies have described favorable effects for RSV, thereby
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=154 y=375) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
supporting ?ndings from epidemiological cohort and intervention studies. Baur et al. [88]
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=387) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
demonstrated that RSV alleviated the negative impact of a high-calorie diet on overall
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=400) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
health and lifespan in middle-aged mice. RSV increased insulin sensitivity, AMPK, and
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=412) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor-? coactivator-1? (PGC-1?) activity and reduced
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=425) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
insulin-like growth factor-1 levels. Parametric analyses revealed that RSV gave the signi?-
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=438) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cant opposed effects of the high-calorie diet in 144 out of 153 altered pathways. Although
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=450) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
this study showed that increased SIRT1 enzymatic activity by RSV without altering its gene
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=463) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
expression, a different study showed that RSV restored its decreased mRNA levels induced
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=475) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
by hemorrhagic shock in the rat kidney to the control levels [89].
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=488) width=283 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Price et al. [90] reported different RSV dose-dependent activities toward AMPK and
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=154 y=500) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SIRT1 expression. Mice treated with a moderate RSV dose were able to activate AMPK,
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=513) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
increasing mitochondrial biogenesis and function in skeletal muscle, whereas SIRT1 knock-
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=525) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
out mice displayed none of these effects. A mouse model over-expressing SIRT1 mimicked
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=538) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
these effects, demonstrating SIRT1 was required for AMPK activation. In contrast, a high
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=551) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
RSV dose activated AMPK in a SIRT1-independent manner, demonstrating RSV dosage
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=563) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
was a critical factor [90]. These ?ndings indicated that RSV exerted dose-dependent effects
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=576) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on AMPK and SIRT1 signaling pathways.
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=588) width=183 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Wang et al. [4] reviewed 14 cell and 12 animal studies investigating the anti-obesity
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=154 y=601) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
effects of RSV. Their data indicated: RSV increased adiponectin levels; reduced TG levels
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=613) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and lipid accumulation; upregulated AMPK, SIRT1, and SIRT3; downregulated FASN and
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=626) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
PPAR?; ameliorated drug-induced increases in TNF-? production; and activated NF-?B.
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=638) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Animal study data indicated that the anti-obesity effects of RSV in animals were poten-
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=651) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tially mediated through stimulation of fat oxidation and metabolism, together with the
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=664) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
suppression of adipogenic gene expression, such as PPAR, C/EBP?, SREBP-1c, FASN,
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=676) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lipopolysaccharide (LPS), FA-binding protein (adipocyte Protein 2 (aP2)), and leptin. They
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=689) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
also indicated that RSV not only downregulated obesity-induced chronic in?ammation
page=9 block=1004390006 (x=132 y=701) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

Molecules 2021, 26, 453

10 of 22

these targets, whereby RSV exerts anti-obesity effects are shown in Figure 2.
