5. The Effects of Curry/CRC on Obesity
page=10 block=1739653374 (x=132 y=133) width=180 height=6 fontname=MDGIGQ+URWPalladioL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
5.1. Human Studies on Curry/CRC
page=10 block=1152487396 (x=132 y=147) width=144 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
No comprehensive epidemiological studies have yet reported the effects of CRC on
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=162) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
obesity. Future observational human studies may reveal its bene?cial effects on obesity and
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=175) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
other diseases, particularly in Asian populations which have high CRC consumption levels.
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=187) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
However, multiple clinical studies have reported on the health bene?ts of CRC con-
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=200) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sumption. In an RCT in MetS patients with BMIs between 25.0 and 29.9, 44 participants
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=213) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
showed a <2% weight loss after 30 days of diet, 22 patients were treated for a further
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=225) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
30 days with a CRC mixture (800 mg/dose/day of Curcuma longa extract containing 95%
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=238) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
CRC), while the remaining 22 patients received vehicle (400 mg/dose/day of pure phos-
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=250) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
phatidylserine) [91]. These results indicated that CRC treatment increased weight loss
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=263) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
from 1.88% to 4.91%, enhanced the percentage reduction of body fat (0.70% to 8.43%),
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=275) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
waistline reduction (2.36% to 4.14%), hip circumference reduction (0.74% to 2.51%), and
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=288) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
BMI reduction (2.10% to 6.43%) [91].
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=300) width=160 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Data from four clinical studies led Kunnumakkara et al. [92] to conclude that CRC
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=313) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
was effective in reducing anxiety and depression symptoms associated with obesity. CRC
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=326) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
modulated circulating IL-1?, IL-4, and vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) levels to
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=338) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
generate immunosuppressive effects and reduce oxidative stress in obese patients.
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=351) width=362 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A systematic review of seven RCTs examining turmeric and CRC in patients at risk
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=363) width=371 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of cardiovascular disease suggested they had bene?cial effects on serum TG and LDL-C
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=376) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
levels, although no signi?cant differences were found for serum HDL-C levels. When the
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=388) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
analysis was restricted to more homogenous studies based on underlying different disease
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=401) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
categories, a bene?cial effect of turmeric and CRC on serum TC levels was noted in patients
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=413) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with MetS [93].
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=426) width=67 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A meta-analysis of eight RCT reported a signi?cant reduction (WMD: ?4.69 pg/mL,
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=438) width=372 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
CI: ?7.10, ?2.28) in circulating TNF-? levels upon CRC supplementation, suggesting a
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=450) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
bene?cial effect of CRC on in?ammation [94].
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=464) width=201 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A meta-analysis of 11 studies involving 876 subjects (53% women) found a signi?-
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=476) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cant effect of CRC on body weight (WMD: ?1.14 kg, CI: ?2.16, ?0.12) and BMI (WMD:
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=488) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?0.48 kg/m2, CI: ?0.78, ?0.17), respectively [95].
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=499) width=218 height=8 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In an RCT by Saraf-Bank et al. [96], 60 overweight and obese adolescent girls were
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=514) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
randomly assigned to either a placebo or CRC intervention group. Supplementation with
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=526) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
500 mg/day CRC for 10 weeks was found to signi?cantly lower IL-6 levels and oxidative
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=539) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stress markers, suggesting bene?cial effects on in?ammation and oxidative stress.
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=551) width=359 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
However, data from a 6-month RCT in elderly subjects who consumed CRC or placebo
page=10 block=732277370 (x=154 y=564) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
showed that CRC consumption (doses of either 1 g or 4 g/day) did not signi?cantly affect
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=577) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
TG, TC, LDL-C, or HDL-C levels over 1 or 6 months [97]. Data from another RCT, where
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=589) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
30 obese individuals wererandomized toreceive 1 g/day CRC orplacebo, showed that CRC
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=602) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
signi?cantly reduced IL-1?, IL-4, and VEGF serum levels, but no signi?cant differences
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=614) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
were observed for IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IFN-?, epidermal growth factor, and monocyte
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=627) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
chemoattractant protein-1 levels [98]. Thus, different studies show that the CRC effects on
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=639) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
some biomarkers are variable.
page=10 block=732277370 (x=132 y=652) width=133 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
5.2. Laboratory Studies and CRC Mechanisms
page=10 block=1295820487 (x=132 y=674) width=187 height=6 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Several cell and animal studies have demonstrated the bene?cial effects of CRC
page=10 block=585496942 (x=154 y=690) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
on obesity [4]. In a mouse model study, CRC administration for 28 weeks signi?cantly
page=10 block=585496942 (x=132 y=702) width=393 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 11 left:1 upper:1
Molecules 2021, 26, 453
page=11 block=1784908921 (x=1 y=1) width=78 height=5 fontname=LFKAFQ+URWPalladioL-Ital wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
11 of 22
page=11 block=1821580597 (x=497 y=1) width=27 height=5 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attenuated the effects of HFD on body weight gain, glucose disposal, and insulin resistance.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=36) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
CRC also inhibited the expression of lipogenic genes, including NF-?B, SREBP-1c, and
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=48) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
carbohydrate-responsive element-binding protein in the liver, and blocked the effects of
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=61) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
HFD on in?ammatory pathways in adipose tissue [99]. Similarly, dietary CRC ameliorated
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=73) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
diabetes in HFD-induced obese and ob/ob male C57BL/6J mice and further reduced body
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=86) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
weight gain. CRC also decreased macrophage in?ltration in white adipose tissue, adipose
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=98) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tissue adiponectin production, hepatic NF-?B activity, and hepatic in?ammation [100].
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=111) width=380 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In 3T3-L1 cells, Ahn et al. [101] showed that CRC decreased aP2 (a mature adipocyte
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=154 y=123) width=370 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
marker) mRNA expression, increased c-Myc and cyclin D1 expression (well-known Wnt
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=136) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
targets), and inhibited mitogen-activated protein kinase (MAPK) phosphorylation, which
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=149) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
has been associated with 3T3-L1 differentiation into adipocytes. These ?ndings suggest
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=161) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that the Wnt signaling pathway participated in CRC-induced suppression of adipogenesis
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=174) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in 3T3-L1 cells.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=186) width=66 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Wnt signaling activation represses adipogenic differentiation. The transcription factor
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=154 y=199) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
7-like 2 (Tcf7l2) gene encodes a key Wnt signaling pathway effector, and its human homo-
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=211) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
logue, TCF7L2, may be a high-risk gene for diabetes. Tian et al. [102] showed that CRC
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=224) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
attenuated miR-17-5p expression and stimulated Tcf7l2 expression in 3T3-L1 cells. Since
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=236) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
miR-17-5p expression in mouse epididymal fat tissues increased in response to HFD, these
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=249) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?nding suggested that miR-17-5p could be a central switch in adipogenic differentiation.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=262) width=390 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Ejaz et al. [103] demonstrated that in mice fed HFD, CRC reduced body weight gain,
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=154 y=274) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adiposity, and micro-vessel density in adipose tissue, which coincided with the reduced
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=287) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
expression of VEGF and its receptor, VEGFR-2. CRC has also been noted to activate AMPK,
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=299) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
reduce glycerol-3-phosphate acyl transferase-1, and increase carnitine palmitoyltransferase-
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=312) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
1 expression, leading to increased lipid oxidation and decreased fatty acid esteri?cation.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=324) width=386 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Kunnumakkara etal. [92] characterized CRC moleculartargets, including transcription
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=154 y=337) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
factors, protein kinases, in?ammatory mediators, apoptotic regulators, protein reductases
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=349) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and histone acetyl transferase, growth factors, receptors, and adhesion molecules. CRC
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=362) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
may exert its multiple effects via epigenetic regulation, of which the major targets include
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=375) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Nrf2, ?-catenin, NF-?B, p38 MAPK, cyclooxygenase-2, forkhead box O3, inducible nitric
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=387) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
oxide synthase, ROS, cyclin D1, VEGF, glutathione, TNF-?, and extracellular-regulated
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=400) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
protein kinase.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=412) width=65 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Wang et al. [4] reviewed 11 cell-based, 16 animal, and 3 human studies investigating
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=154 y=425) width=370 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the anti-obesity effects induced by CRC and concluded CRC could down- or upregulate
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=437) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
various transcription factors, enzymes, cytokines, and other signaling pathway components.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=450) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
More speci?cally, downregulated or inactivated molecules included C/EBP-?, PPAR?,
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=462) width=394 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SREBP-1c, FASN, ACC, HSL, SREBP-1c, NF-?B, IL-6, and TNF-?. Those upregulated or
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=475) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
activated included AMPK, Nrf2, and SIRT1. How these molecules are involved in CRC
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=488) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
anti-obesity effects is highlighted in Figure 2.
page=11 block=1152488702 (x=132 y=500) width=198 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
6. Discussion
page=11 block=1225927567 (x=132 y=522) width=61 height=7 fontname=MDGIGQ+URWPalladioL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
This review summarized the favorable anti-obesity effects of consuming green tea,
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=154 y=538) width=372 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
coffee, wine, and curry and their principal associated polyphenols. While considerable
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=551) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
human observational and intervention studies have expounded this hypothesis, several
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=563) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
studies have failed to show any bene?cial effects. Such differences may have been due to
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=576) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
confounding factors, e.g., differences in study design, quantifying consumption methods,
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=588) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
beverage temperatures, cigarette smoking, alcohol consumption, and differences in genetic
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=601) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and environmental factors, such as race, sex, age, lifestyle, intestinal microbiota, and
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=613) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
genetic polymorphisms [2,104,105]. Further complicating these issues is the fact that
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=626) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
polyphenols have limited bioavailability as their absorption from the human digestive
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=638) width=392 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
system is restricted; thus, they are predominantly metabolized in the gut and liver [106].
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=651) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In the future, more comprehensive and de?nitive human studies should be performed to
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=664) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
assess the bioavailability of polyphenols and prove the anti-obesity effects of consuming
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=676) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
these foods.
page=11 block=1071933072 (x=132 y=689) width=53 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=12 block=1100288091 (x=153 y=36) width=373 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
These studies also proposed possible mechanisms underlying the anti-obesity actions of these
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=48) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
polyphenols. Figure 2, which has been generated from previous data [1,3,6,33,85,105,107–109]
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=61) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
illustrates putative mechanism showing how EGCG, CGA, RSV, and CRC may exert their
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=73) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
anti-obesity effects. These mechanisms are underpinned by the downregulation/inhibition
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=86) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of adipogenesis, lipogenesis, oxidative stress, and in?ammation and the upregulation/stim-
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=98) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ulation of lipolysis and mitochondrial biogenesis.
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=111) width=218 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Polyphenols are frequently documented with dual pro-and anti-oxidant roles (Table 1).
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=154 y=123) width=372 height=7 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
This table shows that EGCG, CGA, RSV, and CRC upregulate AMPK by stimulating ROS
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=136) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
generation, but downregulates NF-?B by scavenging ROS. A caveat to this table is that not
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=149) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
all studies are necessarily related to obesity. Currently, it remains unclear what factor(s)
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=161) width=393 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
direct these polyphenols to act as pro- or antioxidants; however, differences in cellular
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=174) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
polyphenol and metal ion concentrations, cell types, and the co-existence of other anti-
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=186) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
oxidants may be important factors. Thus, further studies will be required to clarify these
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=199) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
issues. It should be noted that AMPK activation via TNF-? downregulation (Figure 2) is
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=211) width=392 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
based on Steinberg et al. [107]; however, more evidence is required for this observation.
page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=224) width=384 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=12 block=1100288091 (x=132 y=249) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

eral miRNAs are associated with obesity [112], e.g., miR-17-5p, miR-122, and miR-221.

respectively [75,102]. The effects of EGCG, CGA, RSV, and CRC on miRNAs were then

page=12 block=1913386579 (x=132 y=599) width=394 height=6 fontname=LDXCAZ+URWPalladioL-Roma wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
